115	## Post-Review Amendment (2026-06-23, after deep code review)
116	
117	The deep code review (`59-REVIEW.md`) found a BLOCKER (CR-01) in Finding F that this goal-backward pass missed: removing the resolve-side first-publish skew allowance (`resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0`) while the live folder-creation paths (`write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs`, `platform/windows/write_ops.rs`) still embed `0` would fail-close resolution of every freshly-created folder, and would also break existing signed records that embed 0.
118	
119	Resolution (user-selected): the resolve-side strict-equality tightening was REVERTED in commit `0256ea486` (skew allowance + its two unit tests restored; case-8 vector reverted to `valid`, which also resolves CR-02). The forward embed-1 changes in `publish.rs`/`replay.rs` are KEPT. The full first-publish unification (mkdir/windows embed 1 + an existing-record republish migration + strict equality) is DEFERRED to Phase 60, consistent with the ROADMAP note that Finding F "bridges to Phase 60."
120	
121	Amended Finding F status: PARTIAL — embed-1 forward changes delivered; resolve-side strict cutover deferred to Phase 60. Findings A–E remain fully delivered. Phase remains `human_needed` for the three durability/CI gates above.

26	# Phase 59: Code Review Report
27	
28	**Reviewed:** 2026-06-23
29	**Depth:** deep
30	**Files Reviewed:** 11
31	**Status:** issues_found
32	
33	## Summary
34	
35	Phase 59 implements HARD-10 Findings A–F across the FUSE IPNS verify/publish path. Findings A (error propagation in `fs.rs::build_folder_metadata`), B (re-resolve on pointer-identity change in `inode.rs`), C (`VerifyError::Legacy` carrying cid/sequence to kill the redundant second resolve), and D/E (dead-code removal) are implemented correctly and verified across all five legacy callers and the build-folder-metadata key-wrap path. No key bytes are logged on the propagated error.
36	
37	**Finding F is broken and ships a cross-layer compatibility regression plus a fixture/generator desync.** The phase removed the resolve-side first-publish skew allowance (`resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0`) and asserts "all clients now embed 1 on first publish." That premise is false: the live FUSE folder-creation (`mkdir`) path on **both** macOS and Windows still embeds `0` on first child-folder publish, and was not touched this phase. The API stores DB `sequenceNumber = '1'` for any first publish (accepting embedded 0 or 1). Therefore every folder created by the current/live app produces a signed record whose embedded sequence is `0` while the DB returns `1` — which the new strict `embedded_seq == resp_seq` check now classifies as `VerifyError::Invalid`. Depending on the resolve site this fails the operation or hard-fails folder-key resolution. The TS SDK resolve side still retains the skew allowance, so this is a Rust-only regression that bricks resolution of records the Rust app itself writes.
38	
39	## Critical Issues
40	
41	### CR-01: Strict sequence equality rejects first-publish records the live FUSE app still writes with embedded seq 0
42	
43	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:112` (with `crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:174` and `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:202`)
44	
45	**Issue:** Finding F removed the skew allowance in `bind_verified`:
46	
47	```rust
48	let seq_ok = embedded_seq == resp_seq;   // was: embedded_seq == resp_seq || (resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0)
49	```
50	
51	The justification in the comment (verify.rs:104-107) claims "FUSE now embeds 1 on first publish ... All clients now embed 1 on first publish, so the skew window no longer exists." This is **not true for the live folder-creation path**. The live mkdir child-folder first publish still embeds `0`:
52	
53	- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:174` — `create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, 0, 86_400_000)` (macOS, unchanged this phase)
54	- `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:202` — `create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, 0, 86_400_000)` (Windows, unchanged this phase)
55	
56	The API stores DB `sequenceNumber = '1'` for any first publish regardless of whether the client embedded 0 or 1 (`apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts:280-285` accepts embedded ∈ {0,1}; line 362 unconditionally stores `'1'`; the stored `signedRecord` is the client's record embedding 0). On a subsequent resolve of such a folder: `resp.sequence_number == "1"`, `embedded_seq == 0`, so `0 == 1` is false → `bind_verified` returns `VerifyError::Invalid`.
57	
58	Consequences by site (all routing through `resolve_ipns_verified`):
59	- `replay.rs::resolve_folder_key` (line 349) — `Invalid` is a hard fail-closed: `return Err(...)` aborts folder-key resolution for any vault containing a folder created by the live app, breaking replay of that vault's journal entries.
60	- `metadata.rs`, `events.rs`, `content_ops.rs`, `fs.rs` FilePointer resolve — `Invalid` fails the specific operation; folder metadata refresh / writes against a freshly-created subfolder fail until a second (seq-2) publish happens to land.
61	
62	This only affects the **single-generation window** (DB seq == 1) — once any seq-2 publish occurs, embedded and DB agree at 2. But that window is exactly the lifetime of a newly created folder before its first mutation, and replay (resolve_folder_key) walks ALL folders in the tree, so one new folder anywhere can fail the whole BFS.
63	
64	The TS SDK resolve path (`packages/sdk-core/src/ipns/index.ts:285-287`) still has the allowance, confirming the cross-layer contract was NOT unified — only the Rust resolve side was tightened.
65	
66	**Fix:** Either (a) revert the skew-allowance removal in `bind_verified` until the live mkdir paths are migrated to embed 1, or (b) within this phase, also change the live first-publish embed to 1 to match `next_file_publish_sequence`:
67	
68	```rust
69	// crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:174 and
70	// crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:202
71	let record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(
72	    &ipns_key_arr, &value, 1, 86_400_000,   // was 0
73	)?;
74	// and the matching coordinator.record_publish(&ipns_name_clone, 1);  // was 0
75	```
76	
77	Option (b) alone is still insufficient for records already written with embedded 0 by prior app versions in the field — those remain unresolvable under strict equality. Given the existing-record risk the phase context explicitly flagged, the safe fix is to keep the skew allowance (option a) OR scope strict equality to records the binding can prove are post-migration. Do not ship strict equality while any writer embeds 0.
78	
79	### CR-02: Vector generator is desynced from the committed fixture and the Rust test — regenerating overwrites the new expectation and breaks CI
80	
81	**File:** `scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts:357-368` and `:378-387`
82	
83	**Issue:** The Rust cross-language test (`crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs:170`) and the committed fixture (`tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json` case 8) were updated for Finding F to expect `expected_result: "invalid"` for the first-publish-skew vector. But the generator that *produces* `verify.json` was NOT updated: it still emits `expected_result: 'valid'` for case 8 (line 366), and its sanity-check array (line 378-387) still asserts the eighth result is `'valid'` (line 386). The case-8 doc comment (lines 342-351) also still describes the old "must accept embedded=0 when response sequenceNumber==1" behavior.
84	
85	Effect: running `npx tsx scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts` (the documented regeneration command, and the only supported way to refresh these vectors) regenerates `verify.json` with case 8 `expected_result: "valid"`, which then makes `ipns_verify_cross_language` fail at the case-8 assertion. The fixture and its generator now disagree about ground truth — the JSON was hand-edited (note the appended "(now rejected...)" text in the committed `description` at verify.json:73 which the generator does not produce). This is a latent CI break the next time anyone regenerates vectors, and it undermines the cross-language parity guarantee the test exists to provide.
86	
87	Note: this finding is downstream of CR-01. If CR-01 is resolved by reverting strict equality, case 8 should revert to `"valid"` in the fixture/test and the generator stays correct; if strict equality is kept, the generator must be updated to emit `"invalid"`.
88	
89	**Fix:** Update `scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts` so the generator is the single source of truth:
90	
91	```ts
92	// case 8 push:
93	expected_result: 'invalid',
94	// sanity array (line ~378-387):
95	const expectedResults = ['valid','invalid','invalid','invalid','invalid','invalid','legacy','invalid'];
96	```
97	
98	Also update the case-8 doc comment (lines 342-351) to describe the strict-equality rejection, and re-run the generator so the committed `verify.json` is byte-identical to generator output (eliminating the hand-edited `description` drift).
99	
100	## Warnings
101	
102	### WR-01: Stale/misleading comment claims DB-authoritative return tolerates a "benign first-publish skew" that no longer exists
103	
104	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:126-129`
105	
106	**Issue:** After removing the skew allowance, the surviving comment block still reads: "the binding above guarantees resp_seq == embedded_seq except for the benign first-publish skew, where resp_seq (1) is the correct forward base." With strict equality there is no longer any tolerated skew — the comment contradicts the code two lines above it and will mislead the next reader about whether embedded/resp can differ. (It also implicitly documents the very compatibility gap CR-01 describes.)
107	
108	**Fix:** Replace with: "the binding above guarantees `resp_seq == embedded_seq`, so returning `resp_seq` is equivalent to returning `embedded_seq`; we return the DB value because downstream forward math keys off the API's DB counter."
109	
110	### WR-02: Replay child-folder publish embeds 1 but logs/comments still say "seq 0", and conflict arm comment is wrong
111	
112	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/replay.rs:592-672`
113	
114	**Issue:** `publish_child_folder_metadata` now creates the record at sequence `1` (line 628, correct per Finding F) and `record_publish(child_ipns_name, 1)` (line 666). But:
115	- The doc comment header still says "Publish a child folder's initial empty `FolderMetadata` (seq 1)" in the title yet the body comment at line 675-676 (`replay_mkdir_entry` doc) says "Re-publishes the child folder's seq-0 IPNS record".
116	- The conflict arm comment at line 659 says "Seq 0 should never conflict" while the record is now seq 1.
117	- Multiple log/skip messages elsewhere (e.g. `replay.rs:744-745` "skipping seq-0 publish") still reference seq 0.
118	
119	These are stale-after-edit comments. They are not behavioral bugs on their own, but they actively misdescribe the post-Finding-F sequence and compound the confusion that produced CR-01 (the live mkdir path was left at 0 while replay moved to 1 — an inconsistency these comments mask). Note this also means replay re-publishes a crashed folder at embedded 1 while the live mkdir path would have embedded 0 for the same folder: if the live publish partially landed (DB seq already 1), replay's seq-1 record is an idempotent republish (embedded == dbSeq) which the API accepts — so no conflict, but the two writers are nonetheless inconsistent in what they embed.
120	
121	**Fix:** Update all "seq 0" comments/log strings in `replay.rs` that refer to the child-folder first publish to "seq 1", and update line 659 to "Seq 1 first publish should not conflict". Reconcile with the live mkdir path per CR-01.
122	
123	## Info
124	
125	### IN-01: `is_ipns_not_found` and `classify_resolve_outcome` use divergent not-found predicates
126	
127	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/metadata.rs:211-213` vs `crates/fuse/src/publish.rs:59`
128	
129	**Issue:** `metadata.rs::is_ipns_not_found` matches only `"not found"` (substring, case-insensitive) and deliberately does NOT match bare `"404"` (pinned by the test at metadata.rs:1155-1159). `publish.rs::classify_resolve_outcome` matches `"not found"` OR `"404"`. The two predicates classify the same error string differently: a `"404"`-only error is `NotFound` for replay first-publish detection but a genuine failure for the bin-publish path's `is_ipns_not_found` branch (metadata.rs:502). This is pre-existing and not introduced this phase, but the inconsistency is a latent source of "first publish vs retain" misclassification if the API's error text ever drops the words "not found" in favor of a bare status code. Worth unifying onto the typed `IpnsResolveOutcome` classifier.
130	
131	**Fix:** Route the bin-publish not-found check (metadata.rs:502) through the same `classify_resolve_outcome` predicate, or document explicitly why the bin path intentionally requires the literal "not found" phrasing.
132

## SECURED

**Phase 59 — FUSE IPNS verify/publish hardening and cleanup**
**Threats Closed: 13/13 · Open: 0 · ASVS L1 (V6 Cryptography)**

Every declared mitigation is present in the implemented code at the cited locations. Report written to `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/phases/59-fuse-ipns-verify-publish-hardening-and-cleanup/59-SECURITY.md` (not the repo-root `SECURITY.md`).

### Threats verified

- **T-59-01** (Finding A, `fs.rs:227-228`) — `.ok()` swallow replaced by `wrap_key(...).map_err(|e| format!("Wrap IPNS key: {}", e))?`. No `.ok()` remains on either wrap_key call. CLOSED.
- **T-59-02** (Finding B, `inode.rs:588-611`) — `same_pointer` check on `file_meta_ipns_name.as_deref()` forces re-resolution `(true, None)` on pointer-identity change under unchanged mtime. CLOSED.
- **T-59-04/05** (Finding C) — `VerifyError::Legacy { cid, sequence_number }` struct variant; all 9 Legacy arms consume the carried values; **zero** `resolve_ipns(` calls remain in events/fs/publish/metadata/replay (second-resolve fully eliminated). CLOSED.
- **T-59-07/08** (Findings D/E) — `record_b64`/`marshaled`/`record` scoped into `is_first_publish`; update-publish `None` guard preserved; `signature_verified` field gone (zero refs) with no live signature CHECK removed. CLOSED.
- **T-59-10/12** (Finding F) — first-publish unified to seq 1 in `publish.rs`/`replay.rs`.

### The four security invariants — all confirmed

1. No key material logged on any touched error path (grep across all 8 files is empty; new map_err interpolates only static `CryptoError` text).
2. Verify path stays fail-closed: the restored skew allowance `(resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0)` at `verify.rs:124` is reachable **only inside the `Some(true)` branch** — after Ed25519 verification + IPNS-name binding pass. Forged/unsigned → `None`/`Some(false)`, never the skew. It does not open a fail-open hole.
3. `signature_verified` removal removed a never-read bool, not a check — real verification lives in `verify_ipns_resolve_signature`/`bind_verified`, decision logic untouched.
4. `Legacy`-carried `cid`/`sequence_number` are clones of the same Legacy-classified response (pre-existing D-04 DB-authoritative path); they replace a redundant resolve, not a trust decision.

### Finding F deferral (user-flagged revert `0256ea486`) — verified safe

The strict-equality cutover was reverted because the interactive publish sites still embed 0 (`mkdir.rs:174`, `platform/windows/write_ops.rs` — both untouched this phase). Tightening the resolve side first would fail-close every freshly-created folder. The skew sits behind the signature gate, so anti-rollback stays mitigated; full unification is correctly deferred to Phase 60 and documented at `verify.rs:113-119`.

### Unregistered flags

None. All four SUMMARY `## Threat Flags` sections declare "None" — no new endpoints, auth paths, schema, or dependencies. This is a hardening/cleanup phase that removes network surface and dead code.

No BLOCKERS, no WARNINGS. Phase 59 is clear to ship.

158	+/// Tests for `build_folder_metadata` key-wrap error propagation (Finding A / T-59-01).
159	+///
160	+/// These tests verify that a `wrap_key` failure in the `InodeKind::File` arm
161	+/// returns `Err("Wrap IPNS key: ...")` rather than silently producing a
162	+/// `FilePointer` with `ipns_private_key_encrypted: None`.
163	+#[cfg(all(test, feature = "fuse"))]
164	+mod build_folder_metadata_tests {
165	+    use crate::inode::{FileAttrs, InodeData, InodeKind, ROOT_INO};
166	+    use crate::test_support::make_test_fs_with_keypair;
167	+    use std::time::SystemTime;
168	+    use zeroize::Zeroizing;
169	+
170	+    /// Generate a real secp256k1 keypair via the `ecies` dev-dep.
171	+    /// A zero vec is NOT a valid curve point (wrap_key fails on it).
172	+    fn real_keypair() -> (Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>, Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>) {
173	+        let (sk, pk) = ecies::utils::generate_keypair();
174	+        (
175	+            Zeroizing::new(sk.serialize().to_vec()),
176	+            Zeroizing::new(pk.serialize().to_vec()),
177	+        )
178	+    }
179	+
180	+    /// Build a minimal file inode with `file_ipns_private_key` set and insert it
181	+    /// as a child of root.  Returns the child inode number.
182	+    fn insert_file_with_private_key(
183	+        fs: &mut crate::CipherBoxFS,
184	+        file_ipns_private_key: Option<Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>>,
185	+        file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex: Option<String>,
186	+    ) -> u64 {
187	+        let ino = fs.inodes.allocate_ino();
188	+        let now = SystemTime::now();
189	+        let data = InodeData {
190	+            ino,
191	+            parent_ino: ROOT_INO,
192	+            name: "test.txt".to_string(),
193	+            kind: InodeKind::File {
194	+                cid: "bafytest".to_string(),
195	+                encrypted_file_key: "deadbeef".to_string(),
196	+                iv: "aabbccdd".to_string(),
197	+                size: 100,
198	+                encryption_mode: "GCM".to_string(),
199	+                file_meta_ipns_name: Some("k51file-ipns-name".to_string()),
200	+                file_meta_resolved: true,
201	+                file_ipns_private_key,
202	+                file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex,
203	+                versions: None,
204	+            },
205	+            attr: FileAttrs {
206	+                ino,
207	+                size: 100,
208	+                blocks: 1,
209	+                atime: now,
210	+                mtime: now,
211	+                ctime: now,
212	+                crtime: now,
213	+                is_dir: false,
214	+                perm: 0o644,
215	+                nlink: 1,
216	+            },
217	+            children: None,
218	+            write_generation: 0,
219	+        };
220	+        fs.inodes.insert(data);
221	+        // Register as child of root
222	+        if let Some(root) = fs.inodes.get_mut(ROOT_INO) {
223	+            if let Some(ref mut children) = root.children {
224	+                children.push(ino);
225	+            }
226	+        }
227	+        ino
228	+    }
229	+
230	+    /// Test 1 (RED): A file child with a `file_ipns_private_key` and an INVALID
231	+    /// public_key (zero vec — not a valid secp256k1 point) causes `wrap_key` to
232	+    /// fail.  `build_folder_metadata` MUST return `Err` whose message starts with
233	+    /// "Wrap IPNS key:" and must NOT silently return `Ok` with
234	+    /// `ipns_private_key_encrypted: None`.
235	+    ///
236	+    /// This test is RED under the broken `.ok()` code and GREEN after the fix.
237	+    #[tokio::test]
238	+    async fn build_folder_metadata_wrap_key_error_propagates_as_err() {
239	+        // Zero public_key is NOT a valid secp256k1 point → wrap_key will Err.
240	+        let private_key = Zeroizing::new(vec![0u8; 32]);
241	+        let invalid_public_key = Zeroizing::new(vec![0u8; 33]);
242	+        let mut fs = make_test_fs_with_keypair(private_key, invalid_public_key);
243	+
244	+        let _ino = insert_file_with_private_key(
245	+            &mut fs,
246	+            Some(Zeroizing::new(vec![1u8; 32])), // non-empty private key → triggers wrap
247	+            None,                                  // no pre-wrapped hex → forces wrap path
248	+        );
249	+
250	+        let result = fs.build_folder_metadata(ROOT_INO);
251	+        assert!(
252	+            result.is_err(),
253	+            "build_folder_metadata MUST return Err when wrap_key fails; got Ok instead"
254	+        );
255	+        let err_msg = result.unwrap_err();
256	+        assert!(
257	+            err_msg.starts_with("Wrap IPNS key:"),
258	+            "Error message must start with 'Wrap IPNS key:'; got: {err_msg:?}"
259	+        );
260	+    }
261	+
262	+    /// Test 2: When `file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex` is already `Some(hex)`, the
263	+    /// pre-wrapped hex is carried through unchanged (the already-wrapped path is
264	+    /// not affected by this fix).
265	+    #[tokio::test]
266	+    async fn build_folder_metadata_pre_wrapped_hex_passes_through() {
267	+        let (private_key, public_key) = real_keypair();
268	+        let mut fs = make_test_fs_with_keypair(private_key, public_key);
269	+
270	+        let pre_wrapped_hex = "deadbeef1234".to_string();
271	+        let _ino = insert_file_with_private_key(
272	+            &mut fs,
273	+            None,                                    // no raw private key
274	+            Some(pre_wrapped_hex.clone()),            // pre-wrapped hex present
275	+        );
276	+
277	+        let result = fs.build_folder_metadata(ROOT_INO);
278	+        assert!(result.is_ok(), "build_folder_metadata must succeed when hex is pre-wrapped");
279	+        let (metadata, _, _, _, _) = result.unwrap();
280	+        let file_child = metadata.children.iter().find_map(|c| {
281	+            if let cipherbox_core::FolderChild::File(fp) = c {
282	+                Some(fp)
283	+            } else {
284	+                None
285	+            }
286	+        });
287	+        assert!(file_child.is_some(), "Must have one File child");
288	+        assert_eq!(
289	+            file_child.unwrap().ipns_private_key_encrypted.as_deref(),
290	+            Some(pre_wrapped_hex.as_str()),
291	+            "Pre-wrapped hex must be carried through unchanged"
292	+        );
293	+    }
294	+
295	+    /// Test 3: When the file has NEITHER a private key NOR a pre-wrapped hex,
296	+    /// `ipns_private_key_encrypted` is `None` and `build_folder_metadata` returns
297	+    /// `Ok` (the genuinely-absent path stays `None` — only the wrap-FAILURE path
298	+    /// becomes `Err`).
299	+    #[tokio::test]
300	+    async fn build_folder_metadata_absent_key_produces_none_not_err() {
301	+        let (private_key, public_key) = real_keypair();
302	+        let mut fs = make_test_fs_with_keypair(private_key, public_key);
303	+
304	+        let _ino = insert_file_with_private_key(
305	+            &mut fs,
306	+            None, // no private key
307	+            None, // no pre-wrapped hex
308	+        );
309	+
310	+        let result = fs.build_folder_metadata(ROOT_INO);
311	+        assert!(result.is_ok(), "build_folder_metadata must succeed when key is absent");
312	+        let (metadata, _, _, _, _) = result.unwrap();
313	+        let file_child = metadata.children.iter().find_map(|c| {
314	+            if let cipherbox_core::FolderChild::File(fp) = c {
315	+                Some(fp)
316	+            } else {
317	+                None
318	+            }
319	+        });
320	+        assert!(file_child.is_some(), "Must have one File child");
321	+        assert!(
322	+            file_child.unwrap().ipns_private_key_encrypted.is_none(),
323	+            "ipns_private_key_encrypted must be None when no key is present"
324	+        );
325	+    }
326	+}

358	+                                            file_pointer.name

369	+
370	+    // ---- Finding B / T-59-02: file_meta_ipns_name change forces re-resolution ----
371	+    //
372	+    // These tests verify that when a resolved file's `file_meta_ipns_name` changes
373	+    // (same mtime), `populate_folder` marks it unresolved instead of carrying over
374	+    // the stale CID/keys.  The mtime-change path is also regression-guarded.
375	+
376	+    /// Helper: seed a resolved file inode in the table under root and return
377	+    /// the file's ino.
378	+    fn seed_resolved_file(
379	+        table: &mut InodeTable,
380	+        ipns_name: &str,
381	+        mtime_ms: u64,
382	+    ) -> u64 {
383	+        // Initial population with a placeholder FilePointer
384	+        let meta = cipherbox_core::FolderMetadata {
385	+            version: "v2".to_string(),
386	+            children: vec![cipherbox_core::FolderChild::File(
387	+                cipherbox_core::folder::FilePointer {
388	+                    id: "fp-seed".to_string(),
389	+                    name: "report.txt".to_string(),
390	+                    file_meta_ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
391	+                    ipns_private_key_encrypted: None,
392	+                    created_at: 1000,
393	+                    modified_at: mtime_ms,
394	+                },
395	+            )],
396	+        };
397	+        let private_key = vec![0u8; 32];
398	+        let public_key = vec![0u8; 33];
399	+        table
400	+            .populate_folder(ROOT_INO, &meta, &private_key, &public_key, false)
401	+            .unwrap();
402	+
403	+        let file_ino = {
404	+            let root = table.get(ROOT_INO).unwrap();
405	+            root.children.as_ref().unwrap()[0]
406	+        };
407	+
408	+        // Manually mark as resolved with the stable ipns_name
409	+        {
410	+            let file = table.get_mut(file_ino).unwrap();
411	+            file.attr.mtime = std::time::UNIX_EPOCH + std::time::Duration::from_millis(mtime_ms);
412	+            file.kind = InodeKind::File {
413	+                cid: "bafyoriginal".to_string(),
414	+                encrypted_file_key: "originalkey".to_string(),
415	+                iv: "originaliv".to_string(),
416	+                size: 42,
417	+                encryption_mode: "GCM".to_string(),
418	+                file_meta_ipns_name: Some(ipns_name.to_string()),
419	+                file_meta_resolved: true,
420	+                file_ipns_private_key: Some(Zeroizing::new(vec![9u8; 32])),
421	+                file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex: Some("enckeyhex".to_string()),
422	+                versions: None,
423	+            };
424	+        }
425	+        file_ino
426	+    }
427	+
428	+    /// Test 1 (RED): A resolved file with the SAME mtime but a DIFFERENT
429	+    /// `file_meta_ipns_name` (incoming pointer is a different identity) MUST be
430	+    /// marked `file_meta_resolved: false` so stale CID/keys are not carried over.
431	+    ///
432	+    /// This test is RED under the broken code (which only checks mtime) and GREEN
433	+    /// after the fix (which also checks pointer identity).
434	+    #[test]
435	+    fn upsert_children_file_same_mtime_different_ipns_name_marks_unresolved() {
436	+        let mut table = InodeTable::new();
437	+        let old_ipns = "k51old-file-ipns";
438	+        let new_ipns = "k51new-file-different";
439	+        let mtime_ms = 5000u64;
440	+
441	+        let _file_ino = seed_resolved_file(&mut table, old_ipns, mtime_ms);
442	+
443	+        // Refresh with a DIFFERENT ipns_name but the SAME mtime.
444	+        // Bug: the current code returns (true, Some(existing.kind.clone())) here,
445	+        // carrying over stale resolved state.  Fix: must return (true, None) when
446	+        // file_meta_ipns_name differs.
447	+        let meta_v2 = cipherbox_core::FolderMetadata {
448	+            version: "v2".to_string(),
449	+            children: vec![cipherbox_core::FolderChild::File(
450	+                cipherbox_core::folder::FilePointer {
451	+                    id: "fp-v2".to_string(),
452	+                    name: "report.txt".to_string(),
453	+                    file_meta_ipns_name: new_ipns.to_string(), // DIFFERENT pointer identity
454	+                    ipns_private_key_encrypted: None,
455	+                    created_at: 1000,
456	+                    modified_at: mtime_ms, // SAME mtime — only name changed
457	+                },
458	+            )],
459	+        };
460	+        let private_key = vec![0u8; 32];
461	+        let public_key = vec![0u8; 33];
462	+        table
463	+            .populate_folder(ROOT_INO, &meta_v2, &private_key, &public_key, true)
464	+            .unwrap();
465	+
466	+        let root = table.get(ROOT_INO).unwrap();
467	+        let refreshed_ino = root.children.as_ref().unwrap()[0];
468	+        let refreshed = table.get(refreshed_ino).unwrap();
469	+        match &refreshed.kind {
470	+            InodeKind::File {
471	+                file_meta_resolved,
472	+                file_meta_ipns_name,
473	+                ..
474	+            } => {
475	+                assert!(
476	+                    !*file_meta_resolved,
477	+                    "Finding B: file with changed file_meta_ipns_name (same mtime) MUST be \
478	+                     marked file_meta_resolved=false; got true (stale state carried over)"
479	+                );
480	+                assert_eq!(
481	+                    file_meta_ipns_name.as_deref(),
482	+                    Some(new_ipns),
483	+                    "Finding B: new ipns name must be set after pointer-identity change"
484	+                );
485	+            }
486	+            _ => panic!("Expected File kind"),
487	+        }
488	+    }
489	+
490	+    /// Test 2: A resolved file with the SAME mtime AND the SAME `file_meta_ipns_name`
491	+    /// keeps `file_meta_resolved: true` (no spurious re-resolution when the pointer
492	+    /// is unchanged).
493	+    #[test]
494	+    fn upsert_children_file_same_mtime_same_ipns_name_stays_resolved() {
495	+        let mut table = InodeTable::new();
496	+        let ipns = "k51same-file-ipns";
497	+        let mtime_ms = 5000u64;
498	+
499	+        let _file_ino = seed_resolved_file(&mut table, ipns, mtime_ms);
500	+
501	+        // Refresh with the SAME ipns_name AND same mtime — no re-resolution needed.
502	+        let meta_v2 = cipherbox_core::FolderMetadata {
503	+            version: "v2".to_string(),
504	+            children: vec![cipherbox_core::FolderChild::File(
505	+                cipherbox_core::folder::FilePointer {
506	+                    id: "fp-v2".to_string(),
507	+                    name: "report.txt".to_string(),
508	+                    file_meta_ipns_name: ipns.to_string(), // SAME pointer identity
509	+                    ipns_private_key_encrypted: None,
510	+                    created_at: 1000,
511	+                    modified_at: mtime_ms, // SAME mtime
512	+                },
513	+            )],
514	+        };
515	+        let private_key = vec![0u8; 32];
516	+        let public_key = vec![0u8; 33];
517	+        table
518	+            .populate_folder(ROOT_INO, &meta_v2, &private_key, &public_key, true)
519	+            .unwrap();
520	+
521	+        let root = table.get(ROOT_INO).unwrap();
522	+        let refreshed_ino = root.children.as_ref().unwrap()[0];
523	+        let refreshed = table.get(refreshed_ino).unwrap();
524	+        match &refreshed.kind {
525	+            InodeKind::File { file_meta_resolved, .. } => {
526	+                assert!(
527	+                    *file_meta_resolved,
528	+                    "Finding B: file with same mtime and same ipns_name MUST stay resolved"
529	+                );
530	+            }
531	+            _ => panic!("Expected File kind"),
532	+        }
533	+    }
534	+
535	+    /// Test 3 (regression guard): A file with a CHANGED mtime still triggers
536	+    /// re-resolution regardless of ipns_name (the existing mtime path is unchanged).
537	+    #[test]
538	+    fn upsert_children_file_changed_mtime_marks_unresolved_regression_guard() {
539	+        let mut table = InodeTable::new();
540	+        let ipns = "k51unchanged-file-ipns";
541	+        let mtime_ms = 5000u64;
542	+
543	+        let _file_ino = seed_resolved_file(&mut table, ipns, mtime_ms);
544	+
545	+        // Refresh with CHANGED mtime → must force re-resolution (existing behavior).
546	+        let meta_v2 = cipherbox_core::FolderMetadata {
547	+            version: "v2".to_string(),
548	+            children: vec![cipherbox_core::FolderChild::File(
549	+                cipherbox_core::folder::FilePointer {
550	+                    id: "fp-v2".to_string(),
551	+                    name: "report.txt".to_string(),
552	+                    file_meta_ipns_name: ipns.to_string(), // same name
553	+                    ipns_private_key_encrypted: None,
554	+                    created_at: 1000,
555	+                    modified_at: mtime_ms + 1000, // CHANGED mtime
556	+                },
557	+            )],
558	+        };
559	+        let private_key = vec![0u8; 32];
560	+        let public_key = vec![0u8; 33];
561	+        table
562	+            .populate_folder(ROOT_INO, &meta_v2, &private_key, &public_key, true)
563	+            .unwrap();
564	+
565	+        let root = table.get(ROOT_INO).unwrap();
566	+        let refreshed_ino = root.children.as_ref().unwrap()[0];
567	+        let refreshed = table.get(refreshed_ino).unwrap();
568	+        match &refreshed.kind {
569	+            InodeKind::File { file_meta_resolved, .. } => {
570	+                assert!(
571	+                    !*file_meta_resolved,
572	+                    "Regression guard: changed mtime must still force re-resolution"
573	+                );
574	+            }
575	+            _ => panic!("Expected File kind"),
576	+        }
577	+    }
578	 }

Touches durability-critical publish paths, so full SDK-E2E + desktop-E2E gated.

1	---
2	created: 2026-06-23T22:45:00.000Z
3	title: Phase 60 — complete IPNS first-publish unification and strict-equality cutover
4	area: infra
5	severity: medium
6	resolves_phase: 60
7	source: Phase 59 Finding F / code review CR-01 (deferred) — 2026-06-23
8	files:
9	  - crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs
10	  - crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs
11	  - crates/fuse/src/verify.rs
12	  - crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs
13	  - tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json
14	  - scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts
15	---
16	
17	## Problem
18	
19	Phase 59 Finding F tried to unify the IPNS first-publish embedded-sequence convention (FUSE 0 vs
20	SDK 1) and tighten `verify.rs` to strict `embedded_seq == resp_seq`. The deep code review caught
21	that this was a premature breaking change (CR-01): the resolve-side strict equality was applied
22	while the live folder-creation paths still embed `0`, which would fail-close resolution of every
23	freshly-created folder AND break existing signed records that embed 0. The strict cutover was
24	REVERTED (commit `0256ea486`); the skew allowance is restored and the cutover deferred here.
25	
26	Current (post-Phase-59) state:
27	
28	- `publish.rs::next_file_publish_sequence(is_first=true)` returns `1`; `replay.rs:628` child-folder
29	  first-publish embeds `1` (forward-compat changes KEPT).
30	- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:173` and
31	  `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:201` still embed `0` (NOT migrated).
32	- `verify.rs` retains the skew allowance `embedded_seq == resp_seq || (resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0)`
33	  (with a deferral NOTE pointing here).
34	- The TS SDK resolve side (`packages/sdk-core/src/ipns/index.ts`) also still has the allowance.
35	
36	## Solution
37	
38	Land the full cross-layer cutover in Phase 60 (IPNS Verification Cross-Layer Closeout: Desktop + API):
39	
40	1. Change the interactive folder-create paths to embed `1`: `mkdir.rs:173` and
41	   `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:201` (`create_ipns_record(..., 1, ...)` + matching
42	   `coordinator.record_publish(..., 1)`), so ALL FUSE publish sites embed 1.
43	2. Migrate existing embedded-0 signed records: a republish pass (or TEE re-sign) so no live record
44	   embeds 0 before strict equality is enabled. Confirm against staging `folder_ipns` first.
45	3. Only then remove the skew allowance in `verify.rs` (strict `embedded_seq == resp_seq`) AND the
46	   parallel allowance in the TS SDK resolve path — in lockstep, cross-layer.
47	4. Update the cross-language vector: regenerate via `scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts` so the
48	   generator is the single source of truth (set case-8 to `invalid` in the GENERATOR, not by hand),
49	   and update `crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs` classify_vector + the restored skew unit
50	   tests in `verify.rs`.
51	
52	Do NOT enable strict equality on one layer before the other, and not before existing records are
53	migrated — that is exactly the CR-01 regression.
54	
55	Related: Phase 59 `59-REVIEW.md` (CR-01/CR-02), `59-VERIFICATION.md` Post-Review Amendment.

{
  "file_path": "/tmp/pr-body-59.md",
  "content": "## Phase 59 — FUSE IPNS Verify/Publish Hardening and Cleanup\n\nCloses out the Phase 58 IPNS-verification long-tail on the `cipherbox-fuse` crate (requirement HARD-10): finishes the two partially-done durability fixes and clears the dead-code/cleanup debt across the durability-critical files (`verify.rs`, `events.rs`, `metadata.rs`, `content_ops.rs`, `fs.rs`, `inode.rs`, `publish.rs`, `replay.rs`) so the verify/publish/CAS paths carry no swallowed errors and no dead seams.\n\n### What changed\n\n- **Finding A — `fs.rs`:** `build_folder_metadata` File arm now propagates a `wrap_key` failure via `map_err(...)?` instead of swallowing it with `.ok()`. Previously a key-wrap failure silently produced a `FilePointer` with `ipns_private_key_encrypted: None` (durability bug). No key bytes are logged on the error path.\n- **Finding B — `inode.rs`:** a file inode now re-resolves when its `file_meta_ipns_name` changes under an unchanged `mtime`. A swapped remote pointer under the same display name + mtime would otherwise serve stale CID/keys from cache.\n- **Finding C — `verify.rs` + 5 callers:** `VerifyError::Legacy` is now a struct variant `{ cid, sequence_number }` carrying the already-resolved values, so all 9 legacy arms consume them directly. This eliminates the redundant second `resolve_ipns` call in every legacy path and closes the TOCTOU race window (T-59-04).\n- **Findings D/E — `metadata.rs`, `content_ops.rs`:** dead `journal_entry` branch collapsed, dead `current_seq_for_cas` binding removed, `signature_verified` field dropped (a never-read bool, not a check), `is_ipns_not_found` test made legible, unused vector-fixture fields stripped.\n- **Finding F — `publish.rs`, `replay.rs`:** FUSE first publish now embeds sequence `1` (matching the TS SDK convention). **Forward-compat only:** see the deferral note below.\n\n### Finding F is intentionally PARTIAL — strict-equality cutover deferred to Phase 60\n\nThe deep code review caught a blocker (CR-01): tightening the resolve side to strict `embedded_seq == resp_seq` while the live folder-create paths (`mkdir.rs`, `platform/windows/write_ops.rs`) and existing signed records still embed `0` would fail-close resolution of every freshly-created folder. The resolve-side strict cutover was **reverted** (commit `0256ea486`); the skew allowance is restored so existing/new embedded-0 records still resolve safely behind the signature gate. The forward embed-1 changes in `publish.rs`/`replay.rs` are kept. Full cross-layer unification (mkdir/windows embed 1 + existing-record republish migration + strict equality in lockstep with the TS SDK) is tracked for Phase 60 — see `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-23-phase60-ipns-first-publish-strict-equality-cutover.md`.\n\n### Verification\n\n- **Verify:** 6/6 observable truths verified against live source (`59-VERIFICATION.md`).\n- **Secure:** SECURED — 13/13 declared threat mitigations present, 0 open (`59-SECURITY.md`). Verify path stays fail-closed; no key material logged.\n- **Validate (Nyquist):** compliant, 0 gaps (`59-VALIDATION.md`).\n- **FUSE unit suite:** 93 tests pass (`cargo test -p cipherbox-fuse --features fuse`), including the cross-language IPNS verify vector test.\n- **SDK E2E:** 89/89 pass against the real client→API IPNS publish/resolve round-trip — the gate most relevant to the first-publish embed change.\n\n### Human-gated checks (run in CI on this PR)\n\n- `Cargo Check & Test (Windows)` — winfsp-gated counterparts cannot compile on macOS.\n- Desktop E2E (dispatch-gated) — exercises the FUSE first-publish sequence convention.\n\n### Deferred (todos captured)\n\n- Phase 60: complete IPNS first-publish unification + strict-equality cutover (`2026-06-23-phase60-ipns-first-publish-strict-equality-cutover.md`).\n- Pre-existing/info `IN-01`: `is_ipns_not_found` vs `classify_resolve_outcome` divergent not-found predicates — out of phase domain, noted for Phase 60 hot-path work.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"
}

157	    // updates need no re-enrollment. The make_record closure only re-signs (record, cid).

160	    // For is_first_publish, there is no prior sequence to CAS against. The helper
161	    // calls coordinator.resolve_sequence first; for a first publish the server returns
162	    // sequence 0 or "not found" which is handled by next_file_publish_sequence above.
163	    // On a genuine first publish the expected_sequence_number should be None (no prior
164	    // sequence). We only apply CAS for update publishes (not is_first_publish).
165	    if is_first_publish {
166	        // First publish: no CAS expected_sequence_number (no prior record exists).
167	        // D.2: record/marshaled/record_b64 only needed here; build inside the branch.
168	        let record =

170	                .map_err(|e| format!("File IPNS record creation failed: {}", e))?;
171	        let marshaled = cipherbox_core::ipns::marshal_ipns_record(&record)
172	            .map_err(|e| format!("File IPNS record marshal failed: {}", e))?;
173	        let record_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&marshaled);
174	        let req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
175	            ipns_name: file_ipns_name.to_string(),
176	            record: record_b64,
177	            metadata_cid: file_meta_cid.clone(),
178	            encrypted_ipns_private_key: encrypted_ipns_for_tee,
179	            key_epoch: tee_epoch,
180	            expected_sequence_number: None,
181	        };
182	        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(api, &req)
183	            .await
184	            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
185	        {
186	            cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {}
187	            cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict { .. } => {
188	                // On first publish, a conflict means another client raced to create.
189	                // D-01a: no JournalOp::FilePublish variant — return Err → EIO.
190	                return Err(format!(
191	                    "Conflict on first per-file IPNS publish for {} — another client raced",
192	                    file_ipns_name
193	                ));
194	            }
195	        }
196	        coordinator.record_publish(file_ipns_name, new_seq);
197	    } else {
198	        // Update publish: use CAS via the shared helper (D-02 / D-03).
199	        // D.3: guard directly; current_seq_for_cas binding was dead (helper re-resolves internally).
200	        if current_seq.is_none() {
201	            return Err("resolve_sequence returned None for update publish".to_string());
202	        }
203	
204	        crate::metadata::publish_with_cas_retry(
205	            api,
206	            coordinator,
207	            file_ipns_name,

> AGENT

Both code findings verified. My triage:

**Finding 2 (inode.rs, MAJOR) — FALSE POSITIVE, skip.** CodeRabbit claims the pointer-change path returns `(true, None)` and never initializes the incoming pointer's key material. That's wrong: when `same_pointer` is false, line 666 sets `file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex = file_pointer.ipns_private_key_encrypted.clone()` (the *new* pointer's encrypted IPNS key), and `file_meta_ipns_name` = the new pointer's name (line 659). `file_ipns_private_key` is correctly `None` because raw private keys are never carried in folder-metadata FilePointers — they're unwrapped from the encrypted hex on re-resolution. The incoming key material IS initialized. The mtime path it also flags is the same correct block.

**Finding 1 (content_ops.rs, minor) — VALID, fix.** In the `else` branch `is_first_publish == false`, so `current_seq` was assigned `Some(...)` at line 118 — the `current_seq.is_none()` guard at line 200 is unreachable. This completes the phase's own D.3 dead-code cleanup. Low-risk in-scope fix.

**Hydrate the replacement pointer’s IPNS key before re-resolution.**

When Line 605 routes a same-mtime pointer swap into the `was_resolved` rebuild path, the new identity keeps only `ipns_private_key_encrypted`; `resolve_file_pointer` later preserves `file_ipns_private_key: None`, so the file can resolve for reads but lose the raw signing key needed for future publishes. > AGENT

The fresh path unwraps the hex → raw key (line 780), but **`resolve_file_pointer` (lines 885-891) copies `file_ipns_private_key` from the existing inode** — which is `None` after a pointer-swap rebuild — and sets `file_meta_resolved: true` without re-unwrapping the hex. So a swapped-pointer file can end up resolved-for-reads but with `file_ipns_private_key: None`. This looks like it could be **real** — contradicting my earlier CLI dismissal. The deciding factor is whether the per-file *publish* path can recover the raw key from the hex.

Given the stakes (4 Major data-integrity findings on a crypto/IPNS path), I'll launch parallel adversarial verifiers — each reads the actual code paths and returns an independent verdict — rather than trust a single trace.

############ coderabbit-major-inode-586-668-pointer-swap-drops-raw-ipns-key ############
verdict=real_bug  confidence=high  action=fix_now
--- reasoning ---
The finding is correct on its core claim and the key question resolves to BROKEN. Traced end-to-end: the two live per-file IPNS publish consumers read the raw signing key DIRECTLY from the inode's file_ipns_private_key field — the edit/version path (journal_helpers.rs:188-210 → read_ops.rs:862, gated on Some) and the cross-folder move re-encrypt (rename.rs:106, pattern-matched as Some). Neither re-unwraps file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex on demand; only the crash-replay path does, and it is sourced from write-time journal entries, not live state. resolve_file_pointer (inode.rs:884-891) flips file_meta_resolved to true while preserving the None raw key, so the file reads fine but can never publish per-file IPNS updates — a real durability/crypto defect: future edits silently skip the per-file IPNS publish (stale remote record) and cross-folder moves silently skip metadata re-encryption (undecryptable under the destination folder key). On regression ownership: the same_pointer==false→None raw-key handling pre-exists on origin/main, BUT this phase's new same_pointer check (inode.rs:585-612, the only behavioral diff) is precisely what newly routes a same-mtime pointer swap into that key-dropping branch. Before this phase a same-mtime swap kept the old kind (raw key present, albeit stale). So this phase introduces a new path that reaches the defect — it is a phase-owned real bug, not merely pre-existing. The fix is local and low-risk: the replacement pointer's own wrapped key (file_pointer.ipns_private_key_encrypted) is exactly the NEW identity's key, and the fresh path at lines 674-704 is a verbatim template; private_key is already in scope.
--- fix ---
In inode.rs, in the `else if was_resolved` rebuild branch (the same_pointer==false case), unwrap the replacement pointer's wrapped key into the raw key instead of leaving it None — mirror the fresh path at inode.rs:674-704. Concretely, replace `file_ipns_private_key: if same_pointer { prev_ipns_key } else { None }` (line 662) so that when `!same_pointer` it computes the raw key from `file_pointer.ipns_private_key_encrypted`. Suggested shape: before building the InodeKind, add\n\n    let swapped_ipns_key = if same_pointer {\n        None // handled by prev_ipns_key below\n    } else {\n        file_pointer.ipns_private_key_encrypted.as_ref().and_then(|encrypted_hex| {\n            match hex::decode(encrypted_hex) {\n                Ok(bytes) => match cipherbox_crypto::ecies::unwrap_key(&bytes, private_key) {\n                    Ok(key) => Some(key),\n                    Err(e) => { log::error!(\"File '{}': failed to decrypt swapped ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: {}\", file_pointer.name, e); None }\n                },\n                Err(e) => { log::error!(\"File '{}': invalid swapped ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted hex: {}\", file_pointer.name, e); None }\n            }\n        })\n    };\n\n

############ coderabbit-publish-rs-legacy-cas-base-0 ############
verdict=pre_existing  confidence=high  action=defer_todo
--- reasoning ---
The finding correctly identifies a real strict-vs-non-strict asymmetry: on a parse failure with no cache, the non-strict resolve_sequence returns Ok(0) (publish.rs:114-124) while the strict path returns Err (publish.rs:182-184). However this is PRE-EXISTING, not introduced by this phase. The origin/main non-strict Legacy arm already used the exact same `parse().unwrap_or_else(|e| { warn; 0 })` followed by `max(resolved, cached.unwrap_or(0))`. The phase diff only changed the SOURCE of the sequence string — from a second `resolve_ipns(api, ipns_name)` network call to the carried `Legacy { sequence_number, .. }` value — to close a ~1ms TOCTOU race (T-59-04). The 0-fallback semantics are byte-for-byte identical to what shipped on main, so this phase neither introduced nor owns the behavior. Additionally, the realistic blast radius is low: sequence_number is the API's DB-stored numeric string (verify.rs:71), so a parse failure implies a corrupt/non-numeric DB value (practically never). Even then, the publish carries expected_sequence_number=Some("0") and the server enforces CAS — a stale base returns PublishResult::Conflict, and the caller re-resolves and republishes with the correct base (metadata.rs:140-161). So base 0 cannot silently overwrite a newer record; it degrades to a benign conflict-retry, contradicting CodeRabbit's framing of 'publish from an invented base'. The suggested fix (Err on parse failure, cache fallback only when a cache entry exists) is a reasonable defensive hardening and would not break any existing unit test, but it is a pre-existing nit outside this phase's diff. Recommend capturing as a small follow-up todo (mirror the strict path's validation in resolve_sequence) rather than expanding this phase's scope. Verdict: pre_existing, high confidence.

############ coderabbit-replay-record-publish-conflict ############
verdict=false_positive  confidence=high  action=defer_todo
--- reasoning ---
CodeRabbit correctly saw that record_publish(.., 1) at replay.rs:668 runs unconditionally including on Conflict, and named the field correctly. But it is a false positive as filed. First, the fix does not compile: current_sequence_number is a String, record_publish wants u64. Second, the trigger is effectively unreachable: the child IPNS name is freshly keyed and only published after a NotFound resolve, no other party holds the key, and the crashed-session live thread is gone. Third, even if hit, the coordinator max-merges and resolve_sequence takes max(server, cache), so cache=1 vs server=0 only skips a sequence (next 2), never a CAS conflict or data loss. The seq 0 to 1 line is new this phase (Finding F) but the unconditional-record structure is pre-existing and mirrors live mkdir; the embed asymmetry the scenario depends on is already the documented Phase-60 deferral. Not a real durability bug; defer to Phase 60.
--- fix ---
No fix this phase. The literal suggested fix does not compile: Conflict.current_sequence_number is String (types.rs:196), record_publish takes u64 (publish.rs:200), so it needs .parse::<u64>(). The trigger is effectively unreachable and the consequence benign (cache=1 vs server=0 gives a harmless seq gap, not a conflict). If done in Phase 60: move record_publish into the Success arm with seq 1, and in the Conflict arm use record_publish(child_ipns_name, current_sequence_number.parse::<u64>().unwrap_or(1)). On Success recording 1 is correct.
```

With None,\n    /// `resolve_file_pointer` carries None forward (file_meta_resolved flips to true while\n    /// the raw key stays None), so the swapped file can be read but never publishes per-file\n    /// IPNS updates and is skipped on cross-folder-move re-encryption (both read the raw key\n    /// directly from this inode field).\n    #[test]\n    fn upsert_children_pointer_swap_hydrates_new_ipns_signing_key() {\n        // Real ECIES keypair so wrap/unwrap round-trips (a zero key is not a valid point).\n        let (sk, pk) = ecies::utils::generate_keypair();\n        let private_key = sk.serialize().to_vec(); // 32 bytes\n        let public_key = pk.serialize().to_vec(); // 33 bytes (compressed)\n\n        let mut table = the SAME mtime,\n        // carrying the new pointer's encrypted IPNS key.\n        let meta_v2 = cipherbox_core::FolderMetadata {\n            version: \"v2\".to_string(),\n            children: &meta_v2, &private_key, &public_key, true)\n            .unwrap();\n\n        let root = table.get(ROOT_INO).unwrap();\n        let refreshed_ino = root.children.as_ref().unwrap()[0];\n        let for re-resolution\"\n                );\n                assert_eq!(\n                    file_meta_ipns_name.as_deref(),\n                    Some(new_ipns),\n                    \"the new pointer   "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-23T23:40:00.000Z\ntitle: FUSE non-strict resolve_sequence invents CAS base 0 on malformed legacy sequence\narea: fuse\nseverity: low\nresolves_phase: 60\nsource: PR #553 CodeRabbit Major (workflow-verified pre-existing) — 2026-06-23\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/publish.rs\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nIn `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence` (the non-strict path, `crates/fuse/src/publish.rs`\n~114-124), the `VerifyError::Legacy` arm parses the carried `sequence_number` with\n`.parse::<u64>().unwrap_or_else(|e| { warn; 0 })`, then `max(resolved, cached.unwrap_or(0))`.\nOn a parse failure with **no cached** sequence this returns `Ok(0)` — publishing from an invented\nbase — whereas the strict path (`resolve_sequence_strict`, ~182-184) returns `Err` on the same\nparse failure. CodeRabbit flagged the strict-vs-non-strict asymmetry on PR #553.\n\n## Why deferred (not fixed in Phase 59)\n\nA parallel adversarial verifier confirmed (high confidence) this is **pre-existing**, not\nintroduced by Phase 59:\n\n- `git show origin/main:crates/fuse/src/publish.rs` — the pre-phase non-strict Legacy arm already\n  used the identical `parse().unwrap_or_else(0)` + `max(resolved, cached.unwrap_or(0))`. Phase 59\n  only changed the **source** of the sequence string (carried `Legacy { sequence_number, .. }`\n  instead of a second `resolve_ipns` call) to close a TOCTOU window (T-59-04); the 0-fallback\n  semantics are byte-identical to `main`.\n- Blast radius is low: `sequence_number` is the API's DB-stored numeric string (`verify.rs:71`), so\n  a parse failure implies a corrupt/non-numeric DB value (practically never). Even then the publish\n  carries `expected_sequence_number: Some(\"0\")` and the server enforces CAS — a stale base returns\n  `PublishResult::Conflict`, and the caller re-resolves + republishes with the correct base\n  (`metadata.rs:140-161`). So base 0 cannot silently overwrite a newer record; it degrades to a\n  benign conflict-retry.\n\nOutside Phase 59's diff/scope → deferred rather than expanding scope.\n\n## Solution (Phase 60)\n\nMirror the strict path's validation in `resolve_sequence`: on parse failure, return `Ok(cached)`\nonly when a cache entry exists, else `Err(...)` — do not invent base `0`.\n\n```rust\nlet resolved = match sequence_number.parse::<u64>() {\n    Ok(seq) => seq,\n    Err(e) => {\n        if let Some(cached) = self.get_cached(ipns_name) {\n            log::warn!(\"Failed to parse carried IPNS sequence '{}' for {}, using cached seq {}: {}\", sequence_number, ipns_name, cached, e);\n            return Ok(cached);\n        }\n        return Err(format!(\"Invalid IPNS sequence '{}' for {} and no cached sequence: {}\", sequence_number, ipns_name, e));\n    }\n};\n```\n\nNo existing unit test exercises this parse-failure path, so the change is low-risk. Related:\n`59-REVIEW.md`, PR #553 review thread on `publish.rs:120`.\n"
}
